Storybook portable-stories RCE — vitest/node spawning shell, recon or secret-grep child (CVE-2026-27148) AIExploitation Medium
Detects vitest or node test runners (executing Storybook portable stories / composeStories) that spawn a shell, network tool, or process whose command line references credentials, child_process, or the article's PoC marker 'RCE_PROOF'. This is the server-side escalation path: an injected .stories file runs in Node context during testing and pivots to credential exfil or recon. Rationale: The article literally demonstrates the escalation: vitest running portable stories executes the injected story file in a Node context, with the PoC payload containing require('child_process').execSync('id') and printing 'RCE_PROOF:'. Pairing vitest/node parent + storybook-related parent cmdline + child shell OR PoC-string cmdline gives a tight, deployable signature. Did not externally corroborate via WebFetch (permission not granted in this session).
ATT&CKT1059 T1552.001 T1195.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • playwright / test-storybook / chromatic visual regression addons spawning chromium during portable-stories runs (filtered above)
  • Test fixtures that legitimately exec child_process to seed databases or start dependent services
  • CI orchestration wrappers (jenkins agents, github runners) that shell out around the test invocation
  • Developer-authored test utilities that genuinely call execSync for environment setup
  • Coverage tooling (nyc / c8) that spawns helper processes
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "playwright" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "test-storybook" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "chromatic" and ProcessCommandLine !contains "\\node_modules\\.bin\\esbuild" and ProcessCommandLine !contains "\\node_modules\\.bin\\rollup"
Response runbook
1) Triage: capture the vitest invocation context (cwd, branch, story file under test), then grep the repo's src/stories tree for files containing the recon strings the child executed - the on-disk story file IS the payload. Run UC1 against the same host for the corresponding FileWritten event in the prior 24h. 2) Contain: isolate the workstation or CI runner, revoke ALL credentials accessible to the affected user/runner (cloud keys, source-control PATs, package-registry tokens, .npmrc auth tokens), and quarantine the offending branch from being merged. 3) Hunt: pivot on aid/DeviceName for outbound DeviceNetworkEvents/NetworkConnectIP4 in the +/- 30 min window for credential-exfil destinations; check git log for already-committed malicious .stories.* across the org; search EmailEvents / AzureActivity for new OAuth app grants from compromised user accounts in the following 48h.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node","vitest.exe","vitest","npx.exe","npx","pnpm.exe","yarn.exe","npm.exe","npm")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("vitest","@storybook","portable-stories","composeStories","storybook test")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","sh","bash","zsh","dash","curl.exe","wget.exe","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe","whoami.exe","whoami","id","hostname.exe","hostname","nslookup.exe")
   or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("RCE_PROOF","child_process","execSync","spawnSync","AWS_SECRET","AWS_ACCESS_KEY","GITHUB_TOKEN","NPM_TOKEN","/etc/passwd","~/.aws","~/.ssh","env | base64","env|base64")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "playwright" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "test-storybook" and ProcessCommandLine !has "node_modules\\.bin\\esbuild"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentProcess = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildProcess = FileName,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          ChildHash = SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-27148")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-27148")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Hunts the initial-access vector: spear-phishing emails with 'Meeting Invitation' subject containing a URL to the threat-actor staging domain onedown.gesecole.net/download. Catches the email regardless of whether the user clicks. Rationale: Pivots on the article-named subject 'Meeting Invitation', the specific lure filename 'Invitation_Letter_No', and the threat-actor-controlled domain gesecole.net (a domain with no legitimate business use). Triple correlation drops FP to near-zero. Cross-checked against: • https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/ • https://sect.iij.ad.jp/d/2025/02/news-plugx-staticplugin.html
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel team forwarding the Lab52 article body to colleagues (subject contains the lure phrases verbatim)
  • Calendar/SharePoint notifications about external meetings using the phrase 'Meeting Invitation'
  • Phishing-simulation vendors (KnowBe4, Cofense) running an awareness campaign with PlugX-style lures

Suppression filter
| where SenderFromDomain !in~ ("yourorg.com","trusted-partner.com") and not (SenderFromAddress has_any ("phishsim@","knowbe4.com","cofense.com"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull EmailEvents for the NetworkMessageId — confirm SenderFromDomain is external and not on the org allowlist; pivot on UrlClickEvents for the same recipient to see if any user clicked through; check DeliveryAction (Delivered vs Junked). 2) Contain: soft-delete via Defender Submissions, block sender domain at gateway, sinkhole gesecole.net at DNS/proxy, push UrlBlockList for the exact ZIP URL. 3) Hunt: for every recipient, run UC2/UC3/UC4 (MSBuild .csproj, Avk.exe sideload, Run key) over the next 7 days from delivery time; query DeviceNetworkEvents for decoraat[.]net / decoorat[.]net to find post-click C2.

Renamed MSBuild.exe executing inline .csproj from user-writable path AIInstallation High
Detects PlugX initial execution where MSBuild.exe (often renamed e.g. Invitation_Letter_No.02_2026.exe) is launched against a .csproj file dropped in Downloads/Temp/Public. The .csproj carries Base64-encoded download URLs and uses MSBuild as a LOLBIN to fetch the side-loading payload. Rationale: MSBuild is a legitimate developer tool but its location, parent process, and target .csproj path are the differentiators. The article confirms MSBuild is renamed (Invitation_Letter_No.02_2026.exe) AND that the .csproj sits in a user-writable path — the OriginalFileName check catches the rename, the path filter catches the non-developer context. Cross-checks ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName for the rename trick. Cross-checked against: • https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1127/001/ • https://lolbas-project.github.io/lolbas/Binaries/Msbuild/
ATT&CKT1127.001 T1218
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer running MSBuild outside Visual Studio (CLI build of a personal project in Downloads)
  • CI/CD agent installed under C:\ProgramData running build pipelines
  • Power-user with VS Build Tools (not full VS) compiling .csproj from a Git clone under Users
  • Legitimate dotnet SDK invocation that wraps MSBuild against a project in AppData\Local
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("devenv.exe","dotnet.exe","vshost.exe","jenkins-agent.exe","AzureDevOps.exe","Runner.Worker.exe")) and not (AccountName endswith "$")
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the .csproj target file from the host (\Live Response) and grep for Base64 blobs / WebClient.DownloadFile / HttpClient — PlugX .csproj has 3 Base64 URLs pointing to gesecole.net staging. Confirm SHA256 of the loader against 29cd44aa2a51a200d82cca578d97dc13241bc906ea6a33b132c6ca567dc8f3ad (ZIP) or de8ddc2451fb1305d76ab20661725d11c77625aeeaa1447faf3fbf56706c87f1 (.csproj). 2) Contain: isolate device via Defender Live Response, kill MSBuild process tree, quarantine the .csproj + originating ZIP. 3) Hunt: run UC3 (Avk.exe sideload from \Public\GDatas\) and UC4 (Run key 'G DATA') on the same host; pivot UC5 (C2 egress) network-wide for the next 30d.
DeviceProcessEvents | where Timestamp > ago(7d) | where FileName =~ "msbuild.exe" or ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName =~ "MSBuild.exe" or ProcessVersionInfoInternalFileName =~ "MSBuild.exe" | where ProcessCommandLine has ".csproj" | where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\" or FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\" | where not(FolderPath has_any (@"\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\", @"\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\", @"\Program Files\dotnet\")) | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, SHA256 | order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d) | where EventID == 4688 | where (NewProcessName endswith "\\MSBuild.exe" or CommandLine has ".csproj") | where CommandLine has ".csproj" | where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\" | where not(NewProcessName has_any ("\\Program Files\\Microsoft Visual Studio\\","\\Program Files (x86)\\Microsoft Visual Studio\\","\\Program Files\\dotnet\\")) | project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName, TokenElevationType | order by TimeGenerated desc
title: PlugX Renamed MSBuild Executing .csproj from User-Writable Path
id: 7c8e1f4d-2a5b-4d9c-8e3f-1b6a7c0e5d2f
description: MSBuild.exe (or a renamed binary with internal name MSBuild.exe) executing a .csproj file from Users/Downloads/Temp/Public/ProgramData — pattern used by PlugX MSBuild loader.
references:
  - https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.defense_evasion
  - attack.t1127.001
  - attack.t1218
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_image:
    - Image|endswith: '\MSBuild.exe'
    - OriginalFileName: 'MSBuild.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains: '.csproj'
  selection_path:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - '\Users\'
      - '\Downloads\'
      - '\Temp\'
      - '\AppData\'
      - '\Public\'
      - '\ProgramData\'
  filter_vs:
    Image|contains:
      - '\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\'
      - '\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\'
      - '\Program Files\dotnet\'
  condition: (selection_image or selection_cmd) and selection_cmd and selection_path and not filter_vs
level: high
source:(windows.sysmon OR windows.security) (@Image:(*\\MSBuild.exe OR *\\msbuild.exe OR *\\MSBUILD.EXE) OR @CommandLine:*MSBuild*) @CommandLine:*.csproj* (@CommandLine:(*\\Users\\* OR *\\Downloads\\* OR *\\Temp\\* OR *\\AppData\\* OR *\\Public\\*)) -@Image:*\\Microsoft\ Visual\ Studio\\*
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (ImageFileName=/\\MSBuild\.exe$/i OR CommandLine=/MSBuild/i) CommandLine=/\.csproj/i CommandLine=/\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\/i
| NOT (ImageFileName=/\\(Microsoft Visual Studio|dotnet)\\/i)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentBaseFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name=msbuild.exe OR Processes.original_file_name=MSBuild.exe) Processes.process="*.csproj*" by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process_name Processes.parent_process Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.process_hash | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where match(process_path, "(?i)\\\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\\\") OR match(process, "(?i)\\\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public)\\\\.*\\.csproj") OR match(process, "(?i)(Invitation|Letter|Meeting|Agenda).*\\.csproj") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name=msbuild.exe OR Processes.original_file_name=MSBuild.exe) Processes.process="*.csproj*" by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.parent_process_name Processes.parent_process Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.process_path Processes.process_hash | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where match(process_path, "(?i)\\\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public|ProgramData)\\\\") OR match(process, "(?i)\\\\(Users|Downloads|Temp|AppData|Public)\\\\.*\\.csproj") OR match(process, "(?i)(Invitation|Letter|Meeting|Agenda).*\\.csproj") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
PlugX DLL side-load — G DATA Avk.exe running from C:\Users\Public\GDatas\ AIInstallation High
Catches the article-specific DLL side-load chain: the legitimate signed G DATA Avk.exe binary running from a non-installation path (notably C:\Users\Public\GDatas\) and loading the malicious Avk.dll. The numeric two-integer command-line pattern (e.g. '865 322') is a PlugX-specific marker. Rationale: Pivots on three article-specific markers: (1) the exact binary name 'Avk.exe' published by G DATA, (2) the abnormal install path '\Users\Public\GDatas\' explicitly named in the article, (3) the PlugX numeric two-integer argument pattern shown verbatim ('Avk.exe 865 322'). Joining DeviceImageLoadEvents on the side-loaded Avk.dll filename provides a confirmation pivot. Cross-checked Avk.exe SHA256 8421e7995778faf1f2a902fb2c51d85ae39481f443b7b3186068d5c33c472d99 — legitimate G DATA-signed binary, distinguishing factor is location/loader DLL. Cross-checked against: • https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/ • https://sect.iij.ad.jp/d/2025/02/news-plugx-staticplugin.html • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/002/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • G DATA Antivirus uninstaller leaving copies under \ProgramData\ during cleanup
  • Legitimate G DATA support running Avk.exe from a temp diagnostic folder
  • Software-deployment systems (SCCM/Intune) staging the installer under \Users\Public\ pre-installation
  • Forensic analyst manually copying Avk.exe to a sandbox VM
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","msiexec.exe","GDataSetup.exe","AVKWizard.exe")) and AccountName !endswith "$"
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm Avk.exe path is NOT under \Program Files\G DATA\; pull DeviceImageLoadEvents for the same ProcessId and inspect Avk.dll SHA256 — legitimate G DATA Avk.dll has a known hash, malicious Korplug Avk.dll is 46314092c8d00ab93cbbdc824b9fc39dec9303169163b9625bae3b1717d70ebc. Look for AVKTray.dat in the same folder (encrypted payload, ~673KB). 2) Contain: isolate device, quarantine the entire \Users\Public\GDatas\ folder, kill Avk.exe and any svchost-style child processes. 3) Hunt: pivot to UC4 (Run key 'G DATA' persistence) for the same DeviceId, then UC5 (C2 to decoraat.net) across the org — finding one host with this side-load typically indicates 2–5 more in the same campaign.
DeviceProcessEvents | where Timestamp > ago(7d) | where FileName =~ "avk.exe" or ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName =~ "AVK.exe" or ProcessVersionInfoCompanyName has "G DATA" | where not(FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)\\Program Files( \(x86\))?\\G\s?DATA\"") | where FolderPath has_any (@"\Users\Public"", @"\AppData"", @"\Temp"", @"\ProgramData"", @"\Downloads"") or ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\\Avk\.exe[""']?\s+\d{2,5}\s+\d{2,5}\s*$" | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, ProcessVersionInfoCompanyName, ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256 | join kind=leftouter (DeviceImageLoadEvents | where Timestamp > ago(7d) | where FileName =~ "avk.dll" | project DeviceId, DllFolderPath = FolderPath, DllSHA256 = SHA256, DllInitiatingProcessId = InitiatingProcessId) on DeviceId | order by Timestamp desc

PlugX persistence — Run key 'G DATA' pointing to C:\Users\Public\GDatas\Avk.exe AIInstallation High
Detects the article-specific persistence mechanism: a HKCU/HKLM Run-key value named 'G DATA' whose data points to Avk.exe under C:\Users\Public\GDatas\ with two trailing numeric arguments. Single-event high-fidelity registry detection. Rationale: Three article-verbatim anchors: (1) Run-key value name 'G DATA' explicitly named in the persistence section, (2) install path 'C:\Users\Public\GDatas\Avk.exe' (legitimate G DATA never installs to \Users\Public\), (3) two-integer numeric argument pattern from the article example. Any one is high signal; combination is near-certain TP. Cross-checked against: • https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1547/001/
ATT&CKT1547.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Registry
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • G DATA support installer writing a Run key during repair (legitimate path under \Program Files\)
  • Power-user manually re-naming a registry value to 'G DATA' for organisation
  • Endpoint backup-restore tool replaying old registry hives onto a re-imaged host
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("GDataSetup.exe","AVKWizard.exe","msiexec.exe","AVKBackupService.exe")) and InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
Response runbook
1) Triage: read the RegistryValueData fully — confirm path is \Users\Public\GDatas\ (NOT \Program Files\G DATA\). Identify InitiatingProcessFileName — should NOT be a G DATA installer. 2) Contain: isolate device, delete the Run-key value via Live Response (`reg delete "HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run" /v "G DATA" /f`), quarantine \Users\Public\GDatas\ folder, kill any active Avk.exe processes. 3) Hunt: query DeviceProcessEvents for prior Avk.exe executions on the host (UC3), pull NetFlow/DNS for decoraat.net / decoorat.net / gesecole.net (UC5) for the past 30 days, and check the user's mailbox for the originating 'Meeting Invitation' email (UC1).

PlugX C2 egress — connections to decoraat.net / decoorat.net / gesecole.net AICommand & Control High
Detects DNS resolution or HTTPS egress to the article-identified PlugX C2 (decoraat.net, decoorat.net) and staging infrastructure (onedown.gesecole.net, gesecole.net). High-confidence IOC match; both C2 and staging served the campaign over port 443. Rationale: Pure IOC hit on four article-named domains: decoraat.net (C2 per article 'Communications with: https[:]//decoraat[.]net:443'), decoorat.net (C2 decrypted from PlugX config block per article), gesecole.net + onedown.gesecole.net (staging per article '.csproj contains three Base64-encoded URLs using the domain onedown[.]gesecole[.]net/download'). The article cites both decoraat AND decoorat — one is the article-text C2 and the other is the in-config decrypted variant; cover both. Sentinel KQL uses Computer/SourceHostName/DvcHostname for the host column on each table since DnsEvents/CommonSecurityLog/ImDnsActivity do not have DeviceName. Cross-checked against: • https://lab52.io/blog/plugx-meeting-invitation-via-msbuild-and-gdata/ • https://sect.iij.ad.jp/d/2025/02/news-plugx-staticplugin.html • https://attack.mitre.org/software/S0013/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1573.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel analyst sandbox or VM detonating the Lab52 sample for research
  • Security tool actively probing the C2 (passive DNS, urlscan-style health checks)
  • Malware repository fetching the .csproj/.zip artefacts (VirusTotal, Any.Run)
  • Threat-hunter manually browsing the domain for OSINT validation
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe")) and not (InitiatingProcessAccountName in~ ("defaultaccount","securityresearch"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm InitiatingProcessFileName — TP signal is Avk.exe or MSBuild.exe (article's loader chain), not a browser; check whether DNS resolution succeeded and whether a TCP/443 connection followed. Pull PCAP or proxy logs for the connection if available. 2) Contain: sinkhole the four domains at DNS/proxy/firewall layer immediately, isolate the originating host, kill Avk.exe/MSBuild.exe process tree, quarantine \Users\Public\GDatas\ and downloaded ZIP. 3) Hunt: pivot to UC2 (MSBuild .csproj), UC3 (Avk.exe sideload), UC4 (Run key 'G DATA') on the same host for the past 30d; query the org for any other host that resolved these four domains; check ThreatIntelligenceIndicator table for related IOC pivots.
let PlugxDomains = dynamic(["decoraat.net","decoorat.net","gesecole.net","onedown.gesecole.net"]); DeviceNetworkEvents | where Timestamp > ago(30d) | where (isnotempty(RemoteUrl) and RemoteUrl has_any (PlugxDomains)) or (isnotempty(RemoteIP) and RemotePort == 443 and RemoteUrl has_any (PlugxDomains)) | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessSHA256, InitiatingProcessAccountName | order by Timestamp desc
let PlugxDomains = dynamic(["decoraat.net","decoorat.net","gesecole.net","onedown.gesecole.net"]); union isfuzzy=true (DnsEvents | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) | where Name has_any (PlugxDomains) | project TimeGenerated, Source="DnsEvents", Host=Computer, ClientIP, Domain=Name, ResolvedIP=tostring(IPAddresses)), (CommonSecurityLog | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) | where DestinationDnsDomain has_any (PlugxDomains) or RequestURL has_any (PlugxDomains) | project TimeGenerated, Source="CommonSecurityLog", Host=SourceHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, Domain=coalesce(DestinationDnsDomain, RequestURL), ResolvedIP=DestinationIP), (ImDnsActivity | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) | where DnsQuery has_any (PlugxDomains) | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImDnsActivity", Host=DvcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Domain=DnsQuery, ResolvedIP=""), (ImWebSession | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) | where Url has_any (PlugxDomains) or HttpHostName has_any (PlugxDomains) | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImWebSession", Host=DvcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Domain=coalesce(HttpHostName, Url), ResolvedIP=DstIpAddr) | order by TimeGenerated desc
title: PlugX C2 / Staging Domain Resolution (decoraat / decoorat / gesecole)
id: a1b2c3d4-7e8f-4a9b-8c1d-5e6f7a8b9c0d
description: DNS resolution or HTTP(S) request to article-identified PlugX command-and-control (decoraat.net, decoorat.net) and staging (gesecole.net, onedown.gesecole.net) domains.

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.

// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent

| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")

   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"

          NewProcessName, CommandLine

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e7ed0cd4115f3ff35c38d36cc50c6a13eba2d845554439a36108789cd1e05b17", "46314092c8d00ab93cbbdc824b9fc39dec9303169163b9625bae3b1717d70ebc", "8421e7995778faf1f2a902fb2c51d85ae39481f443b7b3186068d5c33c472d99", "29cd44aa2a51a200d82cca578d97dc13241bc906ea6a33b132c6ca567dc8f3ad", "de8ddc2451fb1305d76ab20661725d11c77625aeeaa1447faf3fbf56706c87f1", "5f9af68db10b029453264cfc9b8eee4265549a2855bb79668ccfc571fb11f5fc", "d293ded5a63679b81556d2c622c78be6253f500b6751d4eeb271e6500a23b21e", "6df8649bf4e233ee86a896ee8e5a3b3179c168ef927ac9283b945186f8629ee7", "1151100a0aa1ed88f7897709444fd3b3b1044c10", "2336c9a20ecd53ec1be468282bae94c8160eb93a", "ad833604d230b241e180950980ea462b3812f82a", "d1a86ed06b18efef5ce724d2129cf1583b779b44", "f06da8e29c3f0fafabfc3a524ae8b21730b57ed3", "381247c1d4c68a406237d7d3aa030930", "769687f93869a70511aac1ef7c752455", "7a75e713db41c28378e823322fdea0fd", "9f331a11a054f33664fe86543fc34cf0", "e7cb954f4bbdbadbd2c0206577621683"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2022-20775 — Cisco SD-WAN Path Traversal Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-02-25 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco SD-WAN CLI contains a path traversal vulnerability that could allow an authenticated local attacker to gain elevated privileges via improper access controls on commands within the application CLI. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to execute arbitrary commands as the root user. Vendor: Cisco, Product: SD-WAN. Federal patch due: 2026-02-27.

| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2022-20775")

| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,

// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2022-20775")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId

          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-20127 — Cisco Catalyst SD-WAN Controller and Manager Authentication Bypass Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-02-25 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco Catalyst SD-WAN Controller, formerly SD-WAN vSmart, and Cisco Catalyst SD-WAN Manager, formerly SD-WAN vManage, contain an authentication bypass vulnerability could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to bypass authentication and obtain administrative privileges on an affected system. This vulnerability exists because the peering authentication mechanism in an affected system is not working properly. An attacker could exploit this vulnerability by sending crafted requests to an affected system. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to log in to an affected Cisco Catalyst SD-WAN Controller as an internal, high-privileged, non-root user account. Using this account, the attacker could access NETCONF, which would then allow the attacker to manipulate network configuration for the SD-WAN fabric. Vendor: Cisco, Product: Catalyst SD-WAN Controller and Manager. Federal patch due: 2026-02-27.

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Astro SSRF (CVE-2026-25545) — Node.js egress fetch for /404.html or /500.html with UA 'node' AIExploitation High
Hunts the server-side fetch the Astro SSR engine emits when its prerenderedErrorPageFetch() is hijacked via a spoofed Host header. The Astro server (Node.js) will egress an HTTP GET to an attacker-controlled host for /404.html or /500.html with the default Node fetch User-Agent 'node' — a fingerprint shown verbatim in the Aikido write-up and reproducible from the GHSA-qq67-mvv5-fw3g PoC. This is the SSRF call going out, before the attacker's redirect pivots it to an internal target. Rationale: Article-specific indicators: the Aikido write-up shows the exact outbound request the vulnerable Astro server emits — GET /404.html (or /500.html) with User-Agent: node — and the GHSA-qq67-mvv5-fw3g advisory plus the GitLab advisory entry confirm the PoC behavior. The combination of Node UA + the specific URI suffix + non-private destination is extremely high-fidelity because no legitimate Astro deployment should be requesting another host's error pages. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/withastro/astro/security/advisories/GHSA-qq67-mvv5-fw3g • https://advisories.gitlab.com/pkg/npm/@astrojs/node/CVE-2026-25545/ • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-25545 • https://www.sentinelone.com/vulnerability-database/cve-2026-25545/
ATT&CKT1190 T1071.001 T1133
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
// CVE-2026-25545 — Astro Node server egressing to external host fetching /404.html or /500.html
let astro_hosts = dynamic(["node.exe","node"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ (astro_hosts)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public"
| where ActionType in ("ConnectionSuccess","HttpConnectionInspected","ConnectionAttempt")
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("/404.html","/500.html") or RemotePort in (80, 443)
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("astro","@astrojs/node","dist/server","server/entry.mjs")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, ActionType
| order by Timestamp desc
// CVE-2026-25545 — Node.js (Astro SSR) egress fetch for /404.html or /500.html with UA 'node'
ImWebSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where HttpMethod =~ "GET"
| where HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent in~ ("node","node-fetch","undici")
   or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent startswith "node/"
   or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent startswith "undici/"
| where Url has_any ("/404.html","/500.html")
| where not(ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr))
   and not(DstIpAddr startswith "127.")
   and not(DstIpAddr startswith "169.254.")
| project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, HttpHostName, Url, HttpMethod, HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent, HttpStatusCode, EventVendor, EventProduct
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 7a8c1d9e-3b4f-4d6e-9a2c-5e8f1c2d4a6b
status: experimental
description: Detects the outbound HTTP fetch that the Astro SSR engine emits when its prerenderedErrorPageFetch() is hijacked by a spoofed Host header (CVE-2026-25545). The Node.js fetch() egresses with default User-Agent 'node' for /404.html or /500.html.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/astro-full-read-ssrf-via-host-header-injection
  - https://github.com/withastro/astro/security/advisories/GHSA-qq67-mvv5-fw3g

author: SOC Detection Engineering

Astro SSRF (CVE-2026-25545) — inbound Host header mismatch with 4xx/5xx response (trigger) AIDelivery Medium
Hunts the inbound trigger half of CVE-2026-25545: an HTTP request to the Astro origin whose Host header value differs from the server's canonical hostname and which returns 404/500 (the status that drives renderError() into the vulnerable prerenderedErrorPageFetch path). Tune by populating the allowed-hosts list with your Astro app's legitimate hostnames; everything else is a likely Host-header-injection probe. Rationale: Article-specific: CVE-2026-25545 requires the attacker to set the Host header to a value the Astro server doesn't own and trigger an error page (article states `curl -i 'http://localhost:4321/not-found' -H 'Host: attacker.tld'`). Joining 4xx/5xx response status with non-canonical Host header at the origin captures the trigger event. Sigma omitted because the detection requires an allowlist comparison (not single-event-shape); Defender KQL omitted because Defender XDR has no equivalent web-tier telemetry for Host header values. Datadog omitted because none of the canonical sources in our schema cover Astro origin web logs. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/withastro/astro/security/advisories/GHSA-qq67-mvv5-fw3g • https://advisories.gitlab.com/pkg/npm/@astrojs/node/CVE-2026-25545/ • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/astro-full-read-ssrf-via-host-header-injection
ATT&CKT1190 T1133
Data sourcesWeb.Web
// CVE-2026-25545 — Astro origin receiving requests with non-canonical Host header that yield 404/500
let _expected_hosts = dynamic(["www.example.com","example.com","astro.example.com"]); // tune per tenant
let AppService = AppServiceHTTPLogs
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where ScStatus in (404, 500)
    | extend HostOnly = tolower(tostring(split(CsHost, ":")[0]))
    | where isnotempty(HostOnly)
    | where HostOnly !in~ (_expected_hosts)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="AppServiceHTTPLogs", HostHeader=CsHost, ClientIP=CIp, Method=CsMethod, Uri=CsUriStem, Status=ScStatus, UA=UserAgent, _ResourceId;
let Proxy = CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DeviceEventClassID has_any ("404","500") or toint(EventOutcome) in (404,500)
    | extend HostOnly = tolower(tostring(split(DestinationHostName, ":")[0]))
    | where isnotempty(HostOnly)
    | where HostOnly !in~ (_expected_hosts)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="CommonSecurityLog", HostHeader=DestinationHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, Method=RequestMethod, Uri=RequestURL, Status=toint(EventOutcome), UA=RequestClientApplication, _ResourceId;
union isfuzzy=true AppService, Proxy
| summarize Count=count(), FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated), SampleUris=make_set(Uri, 10), SampleUAs=make_set(UA, 5), ClientIPs=make_set(ClientIP, 20) by HostHeader, Status, Source
| where Count >= 1
| order by LastSeen desc

| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")

| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Claude Code Security: A Welcome Evolution in the Remediation Loop

Snyk
2026-02-23 1 use case 1 technique 2 kill-chain phases detected

Anthropic's Claude Code Security marks a major shift in vulnerability discovery, but AI-driven development requires more than just reasoning to remain secure. Learn how Snyk’s AI Security Fabric integrates with Claude to close the loop between finding vulnerabilities and fixing them at scale.

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.

CISA KEV

RoundCube Webmail contains a deserialization of untrusted data vulnerability that allows remote code execution by authenticated users because the _from parameter in a URL is not validated in program/actions/settings/upload.php. Vendor: Roundcube, Product: Webmail. Federal patch due: 2026-03-13.
